## 1. Lifting The Maximally-entangledness Assumption In Robust Self-testing For Synchronous Games

- **Score**: 9.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-01
- **Authors**: Matthijs Vernooij And Yuming Zhao
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-01-2222/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-01-2222/)
- **Tags**: `robust self-testing`  `synchronous games`  `non-local games`  `PME strategies`  `Quantum Low Degree Test`

### TL;DR

The paper proves that robust self-testing for synchronous non-local games under symmetric projective maximally entangled strategies can be lifted to all strategies, and applies this to the Quantum Low Degree Test.

### Motivation

Robust self-testing is a key primitive for device-independent certification, but existing proofs often rely on the unphysical assumption of maximally entangled symmetric strategies, limiting their physical relevance.

### Method

The authors develop a general lifting argument that removes the PME assumption for perfect synchronous games, then instantiate it in the Quantum Low Degree Test to obtain an efficient qubit test.

### Result

Any perfect synchronous game that is a robust self-test restricted to PME strategies is shown to be a robust self-test for all strategies, yielding an efficient qubit test via the Quantum Low Degree Test.

### Conclusion

This work substantially broadens the applicability of robust self-testing results for synchronous games, making them physically meaningful and enabling new efficient certification protocols.

*Keywords: robust self-testing, synchronous games, non-local games, PME strategies, Quantum Low Degree Test*

- **Tags**: `information thermodynamics`  `channels with memory`  `work capacity`  `agents`  `thermodynamic efficiency`

### TL;DR

The paper develops an information-theoretic model of agents as complex self-organizing systems and shows that thermodynamic efficiency requires forgetting parts of past actions, formalized via the work capacity of channels with memory.

### Motivation

Understanding how adaptive agents process information under thermodynamic constraints is central to connecting information theory with non-equilibrium thermodynamics.

### Method

The authors model agents as channels with memory and analyze their work capacity within an information-theoretic framework, deriving conditions for efficient operation.

### Result

They find that agents operating at thermodynamic efficiency must discard some information about their past actions, revealing a fundamental trade-off between memory and work extraction.

### Conclusion

This work bridges information thermodynamics and the theory of agents, suggesting that forgetting is a necessary feature of efficient information processing in physical systems.

*Keywords: information thermodynamics, channels with memory, work capacity, agents, thermodynamic efficiency*

- **Tags**: `networks`  `correlations`  `network nonlocality`  `Bell's theorem`  `bilocality`

### TL;DR

Introduces a sufficient condition and testable criterion called minimal network nonclassicality to certify when network nonlocality cannot be explained by Bell's theorem, with applications to the bilocality scenario.

### Motivation

Network nonclassicality has attracted interest, but there is a need to distinguish genuinely novel network effects from those that are merely Bell-type nonlocality in disguise.

### Method

The authors define minimal network nonclassicality, derive a sufficient condition for being outside the shadow of Bell's theorem, and apply it to the 3-chain (bilocality) scenario, including examples from quantum theory and exotic operational probabilistic theories.

### Result

They show specific correlations in the bilocality scenario are minimally network nonclassical and hence escape Bell's theorem, and they contrast this approach with prior novelty assessments.

### Conclusion

This framework provides a rigorous way to certify the novelty of network-nonclassical correlations and clarifies their relationship to Bell nonlocality.

*Keywords: network nonlocality, Bell's theorem, bilocality, device-independent, quantum correlations*

- **Tags**: `decoherence`  `bosonic quantum error correction`  `multiphoton interactions`  `circuit QED`  `optimal control`

### TL;DR

The paper develops fast bosonic state preparation protocols using multiphoton qubit-oscillator interactions, achieving reduced preparation times for bosonic codewords and robustness in superconducting circuits.

### Motivation

Bosonic quantum error correction requires efficient preparation of rotationally symmetric states, but standard linear interactions are often too slow.

### Method

They introduce an N-photon Law-Eberly protocol as an analytic baseline and numerical optimal control to design faster multiphoton control sequences; they combine different multiphoton orders for arbitrary oscillator control and extend the approach to multi-oscillator states, validating with realistic superconducting circuit parameters.

### Result

Multiphoton protocols substantially reduce preparation times for binomial, cat, and Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill codewords, enable arbitrary control over the oscillator Hilbert space, and remain robust against qubit and oscillator decoherence.

### Conclusion

These findings can significantly enhance bosonic code performance on planar superconducting hardware, an important ingredient for scalable fault-tolerant quantum computers.

*Keywords: bosonic quantum error correction, multiphoton interactions, circuit QED, optimal control, superconducting qubits*

### TL;DR

This paper quantitatively links non-stabilizer resources, entanglement, and CHSH inequality violations, showing that non-stabilizerness must be asymmetric and non-local and providing a representation-theoretic way to build state ensembles with higher violation probability.

### Motivation

Bell nonlocality and quantum resource theories are central to quantum information, but the precise role of non-stabilizer resources beyond entanglement in CHSH violations is not fully understood.

### Method

The authors use stabilizer entropy to quantify non-stabilizer resources, analyze spectral properties of the entanglement spectrum, and employ representation theory to construct ensembles of states.

### Result

They find that non-stabilizer resources necessary for CHSH violation must have a specific asymmetric and non-local structure, and that flatness of the entanglement spectrum affects violation probability.

### Conclusion

The work clarifies the interplay between entanglement, magic, and nonlocality and offers tools for engineering state ensembles with higher CHSH violation probability.

*Keywords: non-stabilizerness, CHSH inequalities, stabilizer entropy, entanglement, quantum resource theory*

- **Authors**: James Allen And William Witczak-Krempa

- **Tags**: `percolation`  `entanglement`  `measurement-induced phase transitions`  `multipartite entanglement`  `entanglement exponents`

### TL;DR

The paper characterizes the spatial structure of multipartite entanglement near measurement-induced phase transitions, deriving exact entanglement exponents via non-unitary conformal field theory and percolation mappings.

### Motivation

Measurement-induced phase transitions exhibit long-range genuine multiparty entanglement with an infinite hierarchy of exponents, unlike equilibrium critical points, but the general structure and relations among these exponents remain poorly understood.

### Method

The authors introduce entanglement clusters and measure-weighted graphs to represent the average spread of entanglement in local quantum circuits, conjecture exponent relations, and compute exact exponents for a 1D measurement-only circuit mapped to percolation via non-unitary CFT, with numerical extension to a 2D circuit mapping to 3D percolation.

### Result

They obtain exact entanglement exponents for the 1D case, numerically verify them, and find the first exponents for the 2D case; the exponents obey conjectured relations of classical dominance, monotonicity, and subadditivity.

### Conclusion

The work provides a firm foundation for understanding multiparty entanglement structure in MIPTs and more general ensembles of quantum circuits, linking entanglement theory to percolation and non-unitary CFT.

*Keywords: measurement-induced phase transitions, multipartite entanglement, entanglement exponents, percolation, non-unitary conformal field theory*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a composable logical gate error to quantify gate accuracy in approximate quantum error correction, and applies it to linear-optical Paulis and Cliffords in approximate GKP codes.

### Motivation

Perfect logical gate implementations are often impossible in approximate error correction, so a single scalar metric is needed that captures both deviation from the target logical action and leakage out of the code space while composing simply across circuits.

### Method

The authors define the composable logical gate error, prove it is subadditive under successive gates, bound it via matrix elements of physical unitaries between approximate logical computational basis states, and use this to sidestep energy-bounded norms in the continuous-variable setting.

### Result

For linear-optical implementations of Paulis in approximate GKP codes, the logical gate error depends linearly on the squeezing parameter and improves monotonically with squeezing, while some Clifford implementations have constant error even in the infinite-squeezing limit.

### Conclusion

The work provides a useful composable metric for analyzing approximate logical gates and shows that exact gate constructions for ideal GKP codes do not always extend to physically realizable approximate GKP codes, emphasizing limitations of certain linear-optical Clifford gates.

- **Tags**: `thermalization`  `Gibbs state preparation`  `commuting local Hamiltonians`  `Davies generator`  `classical Gibbs sampling`

### TL;DR

This paper designs a quantum Gibbs state preparation algorithm for commuting local Hamiltonians by reducing the task to classical Gibbs sampling, enabling efficient preparation at low temperatures and for systems like the Toric code.

### Motivation

Gibbs state preparation is a fundamental computational primitive in physics and statistics, but existing quantum Gibbs samplers often rely on simulating the Davies generator—a Lindbladian thermalization process—which can be inefficient or limited, especially for highly entangled commuting Hamiltonians.

### Method

The authors construct a Gibbs sampler by mapping specific commuting local Hamiltonians to classical Hamiltonians, so that efficient classical Gibbs sampling for the latter yields an efficient quantum algorithm for preparing the Gibbs state of the former, covering 2-local qudit CLHs, 4-local qubit CLHs on 2D lattices, and cases with classical qubits and uniformly correctable quantum terms.

### Result

The reduction replicates state-of-the-art performance and extends to previously inaccessible regimes such as low temperature, and it can prepare the Gibbs state of the (defected) Toric code at any non-zero temperature in polynomial time.

### Conclusion

The work provides a new route to thermal state preparation for commuting Hamiltonians by linking quantum and classical Gibbs sampling, with potential implications for quantum thermodynamics and algorithm design.

*Keywords: Gibbs state preparation, commuting local Hamiltonians, Davies generator, classical Gibbs sampling, Toric code*

### TL;DR

The paper establishes a low-order Bargmann invariant hierarchy that characterizes set coherence, culminating in a universal fourth-order pairwise criterion for deciding whether a family of quantum states shares a common incoherent basis.

### Motivation

Set coherence is a basis-independent relational form of quantum coherence, but it was unclear how much low-order Bargmann data is needed to decide whether a finite family of states is set incoherent.

### Method

The author analyzes Bargmann invariants of increasing order, proves order-specific completeness and failure results in low dimensions, and interprets the hierarchy as a noncommutative moment hierarchy with a dimension-adaptive low-copy quantum computational test.

### Result

Second-order data are complete for qubits but fail for qutrits; third-order data suffice for qutrits but fail in dimension four; fourth-order ordering-sensitive invariants give the first universal pairwise criterion, which yields a complete test for arbitrary finite families, with explicit dimension-four counterexamples.

### Conclusion

The work provides a low-order hierarchy linking cyclic trace invariants to the noncommutativity that obstructs a common incoherent basis, and offers an operational quantum computational test for set coherence.

*Keywords: set coherence, Bargmann invariants, quantum resource theory, quantum coherence, noncommutative moment hierarchy*

### Motivation

A universal framework for jointly measuring multiple localized observables in QFT while preserving spacetime locality and compositionality is still missing, motivating a new axiomatic-probabilistic construction.

Spinor Bose-Einstein condensates are used as an analog quantum simulator for molecular bending vibrations, exhibiting a phase transition witnessed by entanglement and quantum Fisher information.

### Motivation

To leverage spinor BECs as a controllable quantum platform to simulate the two-dimensional vibron model of triatomic molecules and study its phase transition and dynamics.

### Method

Engineer spinor BEC to simulate linear and bent molecular configurations, using the Wigner function to encode molecular configuration and analyzing dynamics via squeezing parameter, entanglement, and quantum Fisher information (QFI).

### Result

### Conclusion

Spinor BECs provide a viable analog platform for simulating molecular bending vibrations and quantum phase transitions, with entanglement and QFI serving as useful witnesses.

- **Tags**: `complexity`  `quantum algorithms`  `Carleman linearization`  `Vlasov equation`  `plasma physics`

### TL;DR

The paper investigates a Carleman-linearization-based quantum algorithm for solving the nonlinear Vlasov equation with Krook collisions, finding polynomial complexity overhead and severe convergence limitations.

### Motivation

The practical applicability of quantum algorithms for nonlinear ordinary differential equations is unclear, especially for physically relevant plasma models with dissipation.

### Method

The nonlinear electrostatic Vlasov equation with Krook-type collision operators is discretized on a (1+1)-dimensional phase-space grid and mapped onto the input of a Carleman-linearization quantum algorithm, with query and gate complexity bounds derived for large grids.

### Result

The quantum algorithm's complexity is polynomially larger than that of the corresponding classical algorithms due to the dimension, sparsity, and norm of the Carleman-linearized evolution matrix, and convergence criteria require dissipation levels far exceeding those from the Krook operator.

### Conclusion

The Carleman-linearization quantum algorithm does not offer practical quantum advantage for nonlinear Vlasov simulations under realistic plasma dissipation, limiting its applicability.

*Keywords: quantum algorithms, Carleman linearization, Vlasov equation, plasma physics, complexity analysis*

### Motivation

Conventional topological order is usually statically fixed by material composition and structure, limiting its tunability for applications; the authors aim to show that mechanical strain can serve as an independent, orbital-selective control knob for topological phases.

### Method

Density functional theory (first-principles) calculations combined with tight-binding modeling are used to compute band structures, orbital contributions, and Chern numbers of a Tc-adsorbed penta-hexa-silicene monolayer under biaxial strain.

### Result

Biaxial strain is found to modulate the topological order and associated functional responses in an orbital-selective manner, enabling a strain-controlled transition into a Chern insulating phase.

### Conclusion

Strain engineering provides a viable route to dynamically and selectively control topological order in two-dimensional materials, opening prospects for tunable topological electronic devices.

### TL;DR

The paper proposes using the micromotion area as a proxy for detecting anomalous Floquet topological phases characterized by a winding number.

### Motivation

Anomalous Floquet topological insulators break the conventional bulk-boundary correspondence based on the Chern number, making their topological characterization and experimental detection challenging.

### Method

The authors analyze the micromotion of periodically driven quantum systems and relate its area in phase space to the topological winding number that determines the number of edge modes.

### Result

They show that the micromotion area can serve as an experimentally accessible signature of anomalous Floquet topology, distinguishing it from trivial or conventional topological phases.

### Conclusion

This work provides a simple and practical tool for identifying anomalous Floquet topological phases in driven quantum systems, with potential applications in cold-atom and photonic experiments.

*Keywords: Floquet topology, anomalous Floquet, micromotion, winding number, bulk-boundary correspondence*

### Method

- **Tags**: `dusty plasma`  `non-equilibrium steady state`  `structural reset`  `impulsive forcing`  `vortex organization`

### TL;DR

A classical dusty plasma under impulsive forcing undergoes structural reset and forms a new non-equilibrium steady state via collective reassembly.

### Motivation

Understanding how complex plasma systems respond to strong perturbations and whether they can recover to a distinct steady state is relevant to non-equilibrium statistical physics.

### Method

A coaxial plasma jet at 1 kV delivers an impulsive force to a vortex-structured dusty plasma; the response is tracked via imaging and velocity statistics across multiple pressure sequences.

### Result

The original two-vortex organization is destroyed but reassembles over 0.4–0.5 s into a new NESS with larger vortex cores, increased circulation, reduced effective kinetic temperature, and a global directional imbalance.

### Conclusion

The 1 kV event defines a structural-reset regime where strong impulsive forcing produces a quantitatively distinct NESS through collective reassembly, unlike irreversible expulsion at higher energy.

*Keywords: dusty plasma, non-equilibrium steady state, structural reset, impulsive forcing, vortex organization*

### Motivation

Moiré superlattices host low-energy phason modes whose role in electron scattering and transport remains poorly understood in systems like twisted bilayer graphene.

### Method

The authors measure resistivity versus temperature in minimally twisted bilayer graphene devices and compare the data with a semiclassical transport calculation that includes scattering from phasons and conventional acoustic phonons.

### Result

They observe a linear-in-temperature resistivity at high temperature preceded by a quadratic dependence, with the linear slope reduced by about a factor of three compared to magic-angle TBG and modulated by recursive band filling.

- **Tags**: `transport`  `active-matter`  `fusion`  `diffusion`  `microswimmer`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces oscillatory active Brownian motion (OABM), a minimal model for sperm motility that adds a periodic angular drive to standard active Brownian motion.

### Motivation

Standard active Brownian motion neglects the intrinsic oscillations from flagellar or ciliary beating, which are essential for describing sperm transport.

### Method

The authors extend active Brownian motion with a periodic angular drive, derive analytical expressions for time-averaged mean-squared displacement, velocity autocorrelation, and transverse excursion amplitude, and infer parameters from human sperm trajectories.

### Result

The model predicts a crossover between short-time ballistic motion governed by swimming velocity and an intermediate regime with a reduced effective velocity set by the angular beat amplitude, and it quantitatively reproduces single-cell and population-level sperm dynamics under control and hyperactivation conditions.

### Conclusion

OABM provides a compact active-matter framework that links measurable flagellar kinematics to coarse-grained transport, enabling description of sperm motility across physiological states.

*Keywords: active Brownian motion, sperm motility, oscillatory dynamics, mean-squared displacement, active matter*

### Method

### Result

- **Tags**: `condensing plumes`  `steam jets`  `turbulent mixing`  `phase transition`  `scaling laws`

### TL;DR

A combined experimental and mean-field theoretical study of the size and shape of condensing steam jets and plumes in unsaturated air, deriving scaling laws for droplet-laden cloud dynamics.

### Motivation

Droplet-laden cloud dynamics arise from coupled phase transitions and turbulent mixing, and a quantitative understanding of how injected steam clouds expand and condense is relevant to atmospheric and industrial plume physics.

### Method

Laboratory experiments on steam-injected clouds in unsaturated air seeded with condensation nuclei are combined with a mean-field theory to derive scaling laws for plume size and shape.

### Result

Scaling laws are established for the cloud geometry, with distinct regimes depending on whether the cloud is confined to the initial momentum-driven region or transitions to buoyancy-driven expansion.

### Conclusion

The work provides a unified scaling description of condensing steam plumes, connecting turbulent mixing and phase-change physics to observable plume dimensions.

*Keywords: condensing plumes, steam jets, turbulent mixing, phase transition, scaling laws*

### TL;DR

The paper reports the first experimental observation of helicon current drive in DIII-D tokamak plasmas, demonstrating an efficient means of driving current in reactor-grade fusion plasmas.

### Motivation

Efficient non-inductive current drive is a key requirement for steady-state tokamak fusion reactors, and helicon waves have long been predicted to offer an attractive, high-efficiency mechanism.

### Method

Dedicated experiments on the DIII-D tokamak were performed to detect and characterize current driven by launched helicon waves in the plasma.

### Result

Helicon current drive was observed experimentally for the first time, providing a proof-of-principle demonstration of efficient current drive in tokamak plasmas.

### Conclusion

Helicon current drive is experimentally viable and represents an attractive candidate for efficient current drive in reactor-grade tokamak plasmas.

### TL;DR

The LHCb collaboration measures the ratio of semileptonic branching fractions of Bc+ decays into J/psi tau neutrino versus J/psi muon neutrino.

### Motivation

Lepton flavor universality tests in B meson decays are sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model, motivating precise measurements of tau versus muon channels.

### Method

Using proton-proton collision data from the LHCb detector, the ratio of branching fractions R(J/psi) is extracted from fits to the decay kinematics of the two semileptonic channels.

### Result

The measured ratio provides an experimental constraint on lepton flavor universality violation in Bc+ semileptonic decays, with quoted statistical and systematic uncertainties.

### Conclusion

The measurement is consistent with (or constrains deviations from) the Standard Model expectation, contributing to global tests of lepton universality in heavy-flavor physics.

*Keywords: Bc+ meson, lepton flavor universality, semileptonic decay, LHCb, branching fraction ratio*

### Motivation

Planar extensional flow measurements are experimentally challenging, so a method to infer extensional viscosity from more accessible shear data would be valuable for characterizing complex fluids.

### Method

The authors establish a kinematic and rheological correspondence between steady shear and planar extensional flow fields, then use it to map shear rheology data onto extensional viscosity predictions.

### Result

Planar extensional viscosity can be recovered from steady shear measurements alone, yielding agreement that illustrates equivalence between the two flow types.

### Conclusion

The equivalence offers a practical route to circumvent the experimental difficulties of direct planar extensional flow measurements in rheology.

*Keywords: planar extensional viscosity, steady shear rheology, complex fluids, constitutive equivalence, non-Newtonian flow*

### Motivation

Spherical harmonic expansions are widely used in geodesy and planetary gravimetry, but their convergence properties and the numerical instabilities of polyhedral planet models are not well understood physically.

### Method

The authors apply rigorous complex-analysis results on the singularities of the gravitational potential to explicit idealized constant-density axisymmetric planet models, deriving convergence regions geometrically without computing expansion coefficients.

### Result

They obtain a novel geometric understanding of the divergence phenomenon, new methods for determining the region of convergence, and an explanation of the fundamental origin of numerical instabilities in polyhedral planet models.

### Conclusion

The complex-singularity perspective provides physical intuition for abstract convergence theorems, and while illustrated on simple axisymmetric planets, the key ideas generalize beyond those restrictions.

- **Tags**: `forest fragmentation`  `land surface temperature`  `climate mitigation`  `remote sensing`  `landscape ecology`

### TL;DR

A global remote-sensing analysis finds that forest fragmentation has a stronger influence than total forest cover on land surface temperature across roughly half of global forests.

### Motivation

International climate mitigation policies and restoration agendas largely target changes in forest area, but the thermal effects of forest fragmentation remain poorly quantified at global scales.

### Method

The authors analyzed global forest cover and fragmentation data alongside land surface temperature observations to statistically compare their relative influences across forested regions.

### Result

Fragmentation outweighs forest cover in shaping land surface temperature over approximately half of global forests, revealing a widespread thermal effect that area-based metrics miss.

### Conclusion

Climate and restoration policies should account for forest fragmentation, not just forest area, to better manage thermal impacts and mitigation outcomes.

*Keywords: forest fragmentation, land surface temperature, climate mitigation, remote sensing, landscape ecology*

- **Tags**: `elastohydrodynamics`  `soft valves`  `mitral valve`  `flow fluctuations`  `biological fluid dynamics`

### TL;DR

A simplified elastohydrodynamic model shows that stochastic flow fluctuations can close a soft mitral valve at roughly one-tenth the pressure required under steady flow.

### Motivation

Understanding how soft valves, such as the heart's mitral valve, close under physiological flow conditions, which are inherently fluctuating rather than steady.

### Method

Theoretical and computational modeling of a soft valve coupled to stochastic fluid flow, using elastohydrodynamics.

### Result

Flow fluctuations dramatically reduce the critical closing pressure compared to steady-flow predictions, by about a factor of ten.

### Conclusion

Stochastic effects are essential for accurate modeling of soft valve dynamics and may explain physiological valve closure at low pressures.

*Keywords: elastohydrodynamics, soft valves, mitral valve, flow fluctuations, biological fluid dynamics*

The authors use a compartmental mathematical transmission model of measles calibrated to Florida's population and current vaccination coverage, simulating scenarios with and without school-entry requirements.

### Result

Repealing the requirements is projected to reduce effective coverage and substantially increase the probability and size of measles outbreaks relative to maintaining mandates.

### Conclusion

Maintaining school-entry vaccination requirements is critical for keeping measles transmission below outbreak thresholds in Florida.

### TL;DR

Srsf3 regulates liver cancer sex disparity by promoting hepatocyte expression of ERα and Foxa.

### Motivation

Sex disparities in liver cancer incidence and outcomes are well documented, but the molecular mechanisms driving these differences remain incompletely understood.

### Method

Conditional Srsf3 knockout was performed in mouse liver hepatocytes and mammary glands, followed by DEN-induced liver cancer and Erbb2-driven mammary tumorigenesis models.

### Result

Srsf3 acts as a tumor suppressor in DEN-induced liver cancer but as an oncogene in Erbb2-mammary tumorigenesis, with differential regulation of ERα and Foxa expression.

### Conclusion

Srsf3 plays a key role in liver cancer sex disparity by facilitating hepatocyte expression of ERα and Foxa.

*Keywords: Srsf3, liver cancer, sex disparity, ERα, Foxa*
